Reviewed research synthesis

Consciousness, Existence, and the Grounds of Significance: A Critical Comparative Thesis

Consciousness Project

Project direction: Michael Nemeth

Prepared with AI assistance in research organization, argument analysis and drafting.

Published with article version 0.4.12 · 2026-09-15. Thesis 1.0.7.

The released thesis, version 1.0.7: internally reviewed and accepted, not externally peer reviewed. Read the full statement.

Abstract

Does consciousness belong to the foundation of reality, and what would follow for existence, survival, meaning and morality if it did? A conscious foundation promises to connect the explanation of experience with the existence of an ordered world, the persistence of persons and the authority of value. This thesis tests that promise by critical comparative synthesis. Six families of positions are set against the same criteria, nine hypothesis components are assessed separately, and every substantive claim is traced through a paragraph-level crosswalk to a reviewed record or a labeled premise. Eight prespecified sensitivity tests, executed by withdrawal at the accepted synthesis stage, are re-read against the chapters. The result is a restricted unification. Experiential primitiveness answers, by definition, the demand that experience enter the fundamental explanatory vocabulary; whether that demand has been correctly identified remains contested, and the move transfers, rather than discharges, the problems of structure and subject individuation. Controlled neural intervention imposes substantive causal constraints on every surviving model, while the selected crisis evidence in no inspected case establishes together the accurate content, the fixed interval and the process information that discriminating extracerebral acquisition would require. Neither an eternal representation of a life nor the desirability of its continuation supplies personal survival. Finite meaningful engagement and practical normativity have resources that do not wait on a settled cosmic ontology, and a benevolent specification must let its goodness restrict what counts as an expected world. The verdict is asymmetric: positive evidence of embodied neural contribution in the tested intervention contexts, and finite significance supported under an explicit value premise, coexist with an unresolved phenomenal explanation and a viable, conditional conscious-foundation programme. Twelve stronger conclusions remain withheld behind recorded gates, and thirty-five evidence gaps receive explicit dispositions. Holistic support remains possible, but only for a constrained joint account whose connections do explanatory work rather than restate the desired conclusions.

Declarations

This manuscript is AI-assisted project research, prepared with AI assistance in research organization, argument analysis and drafting under the direction of the project owner. Chapter drafting, independent review and acceptance follow the project's recorded workflow, in which no author approves their own output and a distinct reviewer reopens decisive sources. Acceptance is methodological. It asserts that a chapter's method was met and its limits stated; it does not assert external peer review, and it does not assert the truth of any metaphysical claim. The research article that this thesis expands never received its contracted final independent review as a single review. That obligation was transferred, without weakening, into the chapter reviews and the whole-thesis review of this thesis, and the release adjudication of 15 September 2026 found the two layers jointly sufficient and released the thesis (state/acceptance_P3G1.json, state/acceptance_P3R20.json, state/acceptance_P3G2.json). The article itself remains an authorized working draft; its promotion to the reviewed presentation is a separate owner decision that has not been taken. Where a chapter states a count or a status as of a named date, it records the position on that date; the current position is the one stated here and in those gate records.

The evidence base is bounded and frozen: publications through 11 September 2026, in English or accessible English translation, gathered under prespecified search and inspection budgets and inspected at identified versions with locators. No payment for access, no contact with witnesses or authors and no request for private records was made. The chapters state, at each point, whether a claim rests on an inspected original, a secondary attribution, a project reconstruction or an uninspected lead, and each chapter closes by saying what it cannot conclude. The reader who rejects a conclusion can locate, through the crosswalk files, exactly which record or premise is being rejected.

1Unification and Its Price

Does consciousness belong to the foundation of reality? And if it did, what would follow for existence, for survival, for meaning and for morality? Those are the questions this thesis asks. This opening chapter expands the first section of the article into a full introduction. It explains why a conscious foundation is attractive. It describes the danger the article calls an illicit economy. It states the restricted-unification thesis that the whole work defends, sets out the nine hypothesis components together with what each of them does not entail, and ends with a map of the chapters to come. The framing comes from two places. One is the project's hypothesis specification, a reviewed stipulative memo that was accepted as method material with every substantive premise left unresolved. The other is the article's own opening paragraphs. Let me say plainly at the start: nothing in this chapter is a finding.

It is worth fixing the epistemic register once, here, so that it need not be repeated. The specification this chapter draws on establishes the existence of no entity, the full coherence of no theory, and the independent verification of no experience. Its definitions and its conditional argument skeletons were written by the project. They are not attributed to any external literature. The article's abstract announces the position that the later chapters must earn. It is an asymmetric verdict: stronger grounds for embodied neural dependence stand alongside an unresolved phenomenal explanation and a viable, conditional conscious-foundation programme. This introduction states that thesis. It does not yet argue it, and no reader should credit any component of the proposal on the strength of the framing that follows.

The promise of unification

Why is the thought that consciousness belongs to the foundation of reality attractive at all? The reason is more substantial than dissatisfaction with materialism. Experience occupies a double position. It is something any account of the world must accommodate, and it is also the medium through which any explanation comes to matter to us. A theory that leaves experience out has therefore done more than omit one phenomenon among many. It has omitted the very phenomenon in which theories are entertained, tested and found significant. That is a gloss on the article's double-position sentence, which is itself the article's opening claim. And the claim is deliberately modest. It says why the proposal deserves examination, not why it is true.

The promise then unfolds in three conditionals, and each of them begins with an "if". If reality is grounded in consciousness, the presence of conscious beings may cease to look like an inexplicable addition to an otherwise complete structure. If that ground also acts intentionally, embodied lives might acquire a place in an intelligible undertaking. If persons endure within it, their attachments and achievements might escape final loss. Notice what is on offer. The proposal promises explanatory and existential unification at the same time: one and the same reality could ground existence, subjectivity and significance. But each "if" is a separate commitment. The distance between those commitments is the subject of this thesis.

The project states the central proposal it will test in a single sentence. Conscious reality is basic; the physical universe and individual experience depend on it; and embodied lives may have enduring significance through a conscious foundation that is not confined to physical succession. That sentence describes a family of hypotheses, and the family needs explicit variants. It is not one claim. Its full and strongest version adds universal subjecthood, personal survival, preservation, intention, goodness and moral authority. Supporting one of those components does not automatically support the whole conjunction. That last sentence is the specification's own warning, and it governs every chapter that follows.

The motivating question is a comparative one. Does such a foundation explain existence, experience, enduring significance, cosmic purpose and morality better than specified alternatives do? The specification makes that question testable in parts. It names components, bearers, packages and disputed bridges. It does so without replacing cosmic purpose with finite-life satisfaction as a substitute target. The thesis asks the same question as the article. The difference is room: room to show the evidence, the reviews and the withheld conclusions in full.

The risk of an illicit economy

Here is the danger, and the article has a name for it: an illicit economy. The proposal offers several conclusions for the price of one primitive, and it can only do so by quietly changing what that primitive means along the way. At the start, "consciousness" names the qualitative character that a purely structural description leaves out. A few steps later it names a unified subject. Then it names a necessary agent. Finally it names a good authority who preserves persons. The word never changes; the commitment grows at every step. The article's own verdict is measured: each transition may be defensible. But notice what has happened. The article's diagnosis is that this is a progression, and each step adds content that the starting point did not contain.

Defensible or not, none of these transitions is merely an unpacking of the original explanatory demand. The article fixes the point with three contrasts. A plurality of basic experiential properties would answer a different question from a single cosmic person. A necessary mind could have intentions quite unlike ours. And preserving everything known about a life would differ from preserving the person who lived it. Whoever affirms the full proposal owes an argument at each of these junctures. The argument owed is different at each.

How does the project guard against this economy? Its instrument is a register of disputed bridges. The register names fourteen inferential transitions. Each is recorded as a premise to investigate, not as an accepted inference, and each is entered with its strongest motivation and its decisive vulnerability. The register begins with the gap between explanatory failure and ontological fundamentality. It runs through the transitions among fundamentality, universality, survival and timelessness. It then reaches the normative bridges: from unity, creatorhood and necessary goodness to moral authority, and from proposed purpose to goodness, which is the bridge whose price chapter 10 reckons. It ends with the convergence bridge, the claim that shared success across several domains strongly supports the full conjunction. The recorded objection to that last bridge is that extra attributes and reused premises can manufacture the appearance of convergence. One rule protects the register. A downstream task that needs a premise not on the list must register a new bridge; it may not call the addition a definitional consequence in order to bypass review.

Four conditional argument skeletons mark the transitions that the later chapters test at length. They were reviewed and accepted as method material, and not one of their premises was resolved. The first runs from preservation to personal survival: retained information plus a subject-preserving identity relation would yield survival. The recorded objection is that preservation can occur while the original subject ceases, and that duplicates expose the identity problem [A-T01-001]. The second runs from endurance to enduring significance: persistence contributes significance only under stated normative premises. The objection is that duration alone need not increase value, and that finite value may exist without persistence [A-T01-002]. The third runs from conscious production to cosmic purpose. Here the occurrence of experience is treated as an assumed explanandum, with no empirical assessment, and a conclusion about intention requires an independently specified end. The objection is that an outcome is compatible with an unintended byproduct [A-T01-003]. The fourth runs from conscious foundation to moral authority: binding obligations require a normative relation beyond descriptive unity, origin, power or reward [A-T01-004]. These are skeletons by design. They are the project's declaration, made in advance, of where the joints that carry the weight are.

The restricted-unification thesis

What, then, is the thesis this work seeks to earn? It is the article's own: a restricted-unification thesis. A conscious foundation is a serious candidate for improving the explanation of experience. Yet its present motivation supports considerably less than a providential account of enduring personal significance. Both halves matter. The first half separates this work from dismissals that treat the proposal as a confusion. The second separates it from defenses that treat the explanatory opening as a down payment on the full package.

It should be said at once what the restriction is not. It is not a demand that every additional commitment carry an entirely independent proof. A suitably constrained joint theory can receive support from shared evidence, and the article concedes this explicitly. The present problem is different. The relevant commitments currently contribute quite different things: an explanatory starting point, disputed modal premises, causal possibilities, interpretations of reports, and normative aspirations. A conjunction of such unlike contributions is not yet a joint theory.

When does convergence become evidence? Only when the connections among the commitments are themselves specified well enough to distinguish the proposal from its alternatives. That is the standard the cumulative chapter applies at the end of this work. It is also why the register's convergence bridge carries the manufactured-convergence objection. A premise reused in several domains and counted once in each, or an attribute added to absorb recalcitrant evidence, imitates the profile of independent support without its content.

The article adds that the comparison should not be neutral by default, and the asymmetries it announces cut in both directions. The selected intervention evidence gives stronger reason to acknowledge neural participation in human experience than to deny it. Yet the inference from such participation to an exhaustive physical ontology is stronger than the evidence warrants. The case against making immortality necessary for every form of meaning is stronger than the case against finding any value in immortality. An assessment can therefore favor some local conclusions while leaving the encompassing ontology unsettled. These are the article's announced positions, and nothing more at this stage. The empirical, meaning and cumulative chapters carry the records that are meant to earn them.

Nine components and what they do not entail

The central discipline of the specification is decomposition with declared bearers. Every commitment must say whose property it is: the foundation's, every person's, some persons', or that of some other explicitly bounded population. The reason is simple. An existential finding, that something holds for some, must not slide silently into a universal claim, that it holds for all. Nine labeled components carry the proposal's content. Each is recorded together with what it does not entail, so that no chapter can spend one component's support on another component's conclusion.

Take them in order. Fundamentality (H-F) holds that experiential properties, or conscious reality, belong to the basic ontology and are not wholly grounded in nonexperiential reality. Two limits are recorded with it. Irreducibility alone does not establish this stronger status, and fundamentality entails neither necessity, universality, cosmic explanation, survival nor intentional creation. Universality (H-U) holds that every subject in the claimed domain depends on one numerically single conscious subject or conscious whole, and the subject-versus-whole variant is made explicit. Shared laws, similar experiences or common ancestry are not this claim. And one whole entails neither timelessness, personal identity with the whole, intention, goodness nor authority.

Brain independence (H-B) holds that a subject can instantiate experience without the bodily or neural processes that the specified dependence model makes necessary. Two distinctions are stated separately inside it: actual occurrence versus metaphysical possibility, and temporary independence versus independence after irreversible death. The component is not the same as independence from all physical substrates. It does not establish that a particular person survives death, that a mind is fundamental, or that experience is timeless. And the absence of a measurement does not satisfy the criterion. Personal survival (H-S) holds that the numerically same person has subjectivity beyond irreversible bodily death, under an explicitly chosen identity relation. Resuscitation does not satisfy the death condition. Under strict numerical identity, resemblance, a stored record or an indistinguishable replacement is not enough. Survival need not be timeless, universal, morally good or unlimited in duration. Timelessness (H-T) holds that the specified bearer has no intrinsic temporal succession. That is not longevity, not eternity through successive times, and not a reported loss of the awareness of time. A timeless foundation does not entail timeless persons, and it does not entail survival.

Preservation (H-P) holds that a specified item from embodied life remains available or has continuing significance. It is recorded separately for information, accessible memory, relationships, subjectivity and value. Preserved information need not preserve a subject. Preserved significance does not by itself entail a storing mind, intrinsic goodness or an intended purpose. Intention (H-I) holds that a conscious agent directs or grounds the universe toward specified ends. Producing consciousness does not by itself establish existing in order to produce it. Intention entails neither benevolence nor success nor authority. And an impersonal teleology remains a separate alternative.

Goodness (H-G) holds that the foundation's nature or agency meets a stated normative standard. Merely having power is not enough, and merely being desired is not enough. Goodness cannot be defined as whatever the foundation happens to do, and preserving experience, suffering included, need not be good on balance. Moral authority (H-A) holds that the foundation grounds genuinely binding reasons or obligations through a specified normative relation. Power, authorship, unity, rewards, punishment or command alone do not establish it. One clarification remains. The components are assessed separately; they are not asserted to be logically independent of one another. Under a survival version with no replacement substrate, H-S entails that instance of H-B. (Survival by a replacement physical substrate, by contrast, may remain compatible with dependence on a brain of some kind.) H-S also entails continuing subjectivity, which is a narrow form of H-P, without entailing complete informational, relational or evaluative preservation. Such consequences must be recorded whenever the relevant version is selected.

Above the components sit named packages. They run from the minimal fundamental-experience thesis, through the conscious-foundation thesis, its timeless variant, enduring personal subjectivity, universal preservation and the consciousness-purpose proposal, up to the full integrated proposal. Two words in the package definitions need care. The word "required" means required in order to count as the named hypothesis; it does not mean established truth. The word "open" means unconstrained; it does not mean denied. Necessity, omnipotence, omniscience, a particular religious identity and unlimited duration for every person are further optional claims, and no package includes them by default. When a required claim is withdrawn or a mechanism is added, a package revision must be recorded. Supporting the minimal thesis after abandoning survival is a possible substantive result. It is not confirmation of the unchanged full proposal. The specification also reconciles the eight rows of the research plan with the nine components. It tracks irreducibility as a weaker subsidiary claim rather than as fundamentality, and it keeps the explanatory target (why anything exists, why physical reality, why these laws, why consciousness) as a cross-cutting question rather than a tenth attribute.

A map of the thesis

Chapter 2 sets out the method. It explains how the project turned this specification into an auditable evidence ledger of sources, claims, arguments and cases. It explains how independent adversarial review, dependence accounting, sensitivity tests and withheld-conclusion gates limit what any chapter may assert. And it explains how the text of the thesis is itself tied to the ledger by a paragraph-level crosswalk. The method is what makes the framing above enforceable rather than merely announced.

Chapters 3 through 5 carry the explanatory arc. Chapter 3 builds the six comparison families of the accepted record: physicalism with its illusionist edge, psychophysical-law and strong-emergence views, panpsychism, substance dualism, idealism in its universal and plural forms, and theism, the last present mainly by stipulation. A fairness rule governs the building. Each family is assessed in the strongest formulation available in the project record, and that is a bounded claim about this record, not about the literature as a whole. Chapter 4 asks what experiential primitiveness would and would not explain, and it holds the line between an unresolved explanatory gap and a conclusion of fundamentality. Chapter 5 follows the proposal upward, from an experiential ground to an intending agent. That is where the questions of existence, necessity and design live, and where the bridge from foundation to mind remains open.

Chapter 6 is the empirical center of the thesis, and it has three parts. The first is the neural intervention evidence that constrains any dependence model. The second is the set of selected crisis-experience dossiers. These are examined at their strongest, and on inspection not one of them establishes three things together: externally accurate content, a defensible interval of acquisition, and adequate information about the processes available during that interval. The third is the frame around those dossiers, namely the prospective studies and the historical and cross-cultural corpus. The chapter's verdicts are verdicts about causal constraint. They are not verdicts about ontology, in either direction.

Chapters 7 and 8 take up continuation and significance. Chapter 7 tests the identity side of the promise: timeless foundations, temporal persons, the ownership of experience, and the difference between an eternal representation of a life and the survival of the person who lived it. Chapter 8 tests the value side. Does significance require endurance? There the article's asymmetry between necessity claims and value claims about immortality is defended, and there the register's production-to-purpose bridge meets the consciousness-factory discrimination design.

Chapters 9 and 10 reckon the price of the proposal's moral crown. Chapter 9 maps where moral authority could come from, and it finds that every route from a conscious foundation to binding obligation crosses a contested normative bridge. Chapter 10 asks what it costs, and what it buys, to specify the foundation as benevolent. It takes up the evidential arguments from suffering and hiddenness and their strongest inspected replies. It then states a constraint: a benevolent unification must let its claims about goodness restrict what counts as an expected world. An unconstrained specification discriminates weakly. It cannot face qualitative tests until its ends, population, means, constraints and tradeoffs are fixed in advance. Both chapters run under a declared symmetry rule. The value premises that support the moral-grounding proposal also underwrite the objections, and neither side may count the same premise twice.

Chapter 11 assembles the cumulative comparison under the convergence standard stated above. Its dependence map and its sensitivity withdrawals keep shared premises from being counted once per domain. Chapter 12 states the conclusions. It also states the withheld conclusions, which are as much a result of this project as the asserted ones. The appendices carry the case tables, the alternatives matrix, the complete register of gap and gate dispositions, and the dependence map. Twelve stronger-conclusion gates remain withheld as of this introduction. Every chapter of this thesis, this one included, ends by saying so: its closing paragraph states that all twelve remain withheld, and chapter 11 lists the twelve conclusions that are not drawn.

Let me be clear about what this introduction has not shown. It asserts no component of the proposal: not fundamentality, not universality, not brain independence, and not survival, timelessness, preservation, intention, goodness or moral authority. It asserts no package, minimal or full. The specification it presents is stipulation. Its definitions are precise enough to classify claims and expose contradictions; they are not measurements of metaphysical structure, and they can be revised only through a versioned change of protocol. The restricted-unification thesis and the announced asymmetries are, at this point in the thesis, claims whose evidence still lies ahead of them. All twelve withheld stronger-conclusion gates remain withheld through this introduction. The chapters that follow either earn them from the ledger or record why they remain open.

2Comparative Method and Evidence Discipline

The method of this thesis is critical comparative synthesis of selected philosophical exchanges and empirical studies. Each word in that description earns its place. The method is comparative because no position under examination can be judged on its own. The question is never whether a view has difficulties, since every sufficiently ambitious view does. The question is whether its difficulties are worse than those of its developed rivals. It is critical because the comparison is conducted against explicit criteria, not by accumulated impression. And it is a synthesis of selected material, not an exhaustive survey. The project's searches, inclusions and inspections were bounded by budgets fixed in advance, and those boundaries are themselves part of the record. This chapter sets out the criteria, the evidence taxonomy, the search and inclusion protocol, the review and acceptance workflow, the dependence controls and the sensitivity discipline. Together they govern every substantive claim in the chapters that follow. The chapter closes by saying what this method cannot deliver, because the honest reach of the conclusions depends on those refusals.

The method was not improvised during drafting. Neither, it must be said, did it come before all of the work it governs. A prespecified evidence and search protocol was proposed as a project task, independently reviewed, and frozen with a recorded hash as work/G0/protocol_v1.md. The evidence tasks that followed were bound by that frozen file and, after the pilot gate, by its numbered successor. The successor takes over the frozen text unchanged and adds seven recorded procedural repairs. The freeze also carries a recorded bootstrap exception, and it has three parts. The three opening tasks that established the project's definitions, its comparison families and this protocol came before the freeze. The backward- and forward-citation passes that the opening comparison task omitted are expressly not counted as executed. And the six comparison families were not thereby source-verified as representative of the literature. Later phases audited coverage against the protocol. Before any cumulative assessment was drafted, they recorded ten binding synthesis constraints, nine component boundaries and eight prespecified sensitivity tests. These are standing constraints on synthesis. They are not a certificate that the protocol was followed without remainder. Where execution fell short of a protocol rule, the shortfall is recorded in the gates and registers rather than quietly absorbed. The method statements in this chapter trace to the frozen protocol, the project execution framework and the recorded synthesis constraints. The thesis crosswalk maps them to those documents as labeled method premises, not as evidence claims.

The criteria of comparison and the place of primitives

Arguments and explanatory proposals are assessed against five criteria. Explanatory adequacy asks whether the proposal actually addresses the problem it claims to solve, rather than redescribing it or changing the subject. Coherence asks whether the proposal's commitments can be held together without internal contradiction and without ad hoc partitioning. Independent motivation asks whether the machinery a proposal invokes has support from considerations other than the problem it was brought in to solve. Fit with the evidence asks how the proposal stands to the observations and reports actually in the project record, the unfavorable ones included. Discriminatory power asks whether the proposal generates expectations that differ from those of its rivals. Why does that matter? Because a view compatible with every possible observation gains nothing from any particular observation. The comparison annex incorporated into the frozen protocol turns essentially these criteria into six labeled checks. The sixth, remaining problems, records what a position still cannot explain after its best defense. In this chapter's terms, that sixth check is carried by the rule about the strongest reply and its remaining cost, described below.

Discriminatory power carries a further discipline. A theory's ability to accommodate a result after the fact is tracked separately from a successful prediction made in advance. The reason is familiar: nearly any developed metaphysical framework can absorb nearly any single observation once the observation is known. So whenever a chapter credits a position with empirical fit, it says whether the fit was predicted or accommodated. The comparison also requires each favored explanation to say what would weaken it, and not only what would strengthen it. An assessment that can only be strengthened is not an assessment.

A stopping point is not objectionable simply because it is primitive. Every sufficiently ambitious theory has basic commitments somewhere. The physicalist takes fundamental physical properties and laws as given. The theist takes a necessary being as given. The panpsychist takes proto-experiential properties as given. The relevant questions about any proposed primitive are three. Does it address the problem it was introduced for, in the sense that positing it genuinely discharges the explanatory demand rather than renaming it? Does it constrain what comes after, so that adopting it has consequences that could in principle embarrass it? And does it impose costs comparable to those its rivals accept, or does it buy its advantages by tolerating what no rival would be allowed to tolerate? These three questions do the comparative work throughout the thesis. No presumption against primitives as such does any of it.

The comparison spans six families of positions: physicalist, psychophysical-law, panpsychist, dualist, idealist and theistic. Each family receives the strongest explicit formulation available in the project record, not a convenient weak representative. Whether that strongest available formulation is an inspected original defender or a weaker stand-in is a separately recorded fact. What it means for what may be concluded is stated at the end of this chapter. Family membership settles less than is often assumed. For each declared model, the project records which commitments are required, which are excluded and which remain open, and these classifications concern the content of a model, not anyone's confidence in it. In particular, moral and identity positions are not inferred from membership in a metaphysical family. A physicalist is not assigned moral antirealism. A theist is not assigned a particular theory of personal identity. Neither is assigned anything of the kind unless the declared model actually includes the commitment.

The object of assessment is taken apart in the same way. The thesis evaluates nine hypothesis components separately: the fundamentality of consciousness, a universal conscious whole, independence of experience from ordinary brain function, personal survival of death, timelessness, preservation, intention, foundational goodness and moral authority. Evidence bearing on one component cannot quietly transfer to another. The recorded component boundaries make this concrete. Irreducibility of experience, or a necessary ground, does not by itself establish one universal subject. Influence of the brain on experience is not the negation of every independence proposal. Fidelity of a preserved record is not personal survival. Production of a cosmos is not intended production. And consciousness, unity, creation, power and endurance do not automatically supply binding moral reasons. Each finding chapter names the components its material bears on, and it respects these boundaries when it says what has and has not been supported.

The evidence taxonomy and the difference between a report and an event

The protocol requires every claim to state its epistemic type, and there are six types to choose from: reported experience, observed event, documented measurement, philosophical premise, normative premise and inference. The type governs what the claim can support. A reported experience establishes, at most, that a person gave an account with a particular content at a particular time. An observed event requires corroboration beyond the report itself. A documented measurement carries its instrument, its timing and its context with it. A philosophical premise is adopted, not discovered. A normative premise is likewise adopted, and it is flagged separately. An inference is only as strong as the weakest step it rests on. There is, of course, a gap between the rule and the practice. The ledger's type fields are free text, and they have drifted well beyond these six labels. The taxonomy binds as a recording rule and a review criterion, not as an enforced schema, and the drift is itself a recorded discipline gap. Why keep the taxonomy at all, then? Because it makes each collapse of one type into another visible as a specific, recordable error.

Three distinctions guide the empirical comparison. The first is between a report and the event it reports. To verify that an account was given, published on a particular date and preserved accurately is not to verify that the events it describes occurred. The second is between an observation and its interpretation. A measured correlation between a neural intervention and an altered experience is an observation. The claim that experience is identical to neural activity, or constituted by it, is an interpretation, and it needs its own argument. The third is between observed dependence and identity. However systematically experience depends on brain function, dependence and identity are different relations, and evidence for the first is not automatically evidence for the second. These distinctions are required by the protocol and checked in review. They are commitments with recorded points of enforcement, not mere announcements. It should be added that the enforcement runs through the judgment of reviewers rather than through any closed schema.

Case evidence is broken down by time. For each case the record separates the medical events and measurements, the inferred timing of the reported experience, the first available account, the later corroboration, the subject's ordinary access to information, and the later revisions of the account. Intervals of uncertainty and outright contradictions are kept as they are, not smoothed away. Three further claims are kept apart because they are so often run together: that information was not in circulation around a subject, that relevant brain activity was not measured, and that relevant brain activity was demonstrably absent. These are three different things. Absence of measurement is never treated as demonstrated absence of processing. And demonstrated absence in a monitored channel is never extended to channels that were not monitored.

Explanatory proposals about particular cases are held to a matching restraint. A possible physiological mechanism is not an established explanation of a specific episode. To name hypoxia or anesthetic action explains a given report only if the proposed mechanism fits that episode's documented timing and circumstances. The restraint cuts the other way too. The credentials and sincerity of a witness do not certify the accuracy of the account, and a financial interest in a story does not establish that the story was fabricated. Each of these considerations enters the record as a fact about provenance with a bounded evidential role. None of them enters as a verdict.

Philosophical arguments are recorded with their premises, their bridge principles and their conclusions kept separate. Each objection is classified by what it targets: validity, premise truth, empirical adequacy, explanatory scope or normative grounding. The strongest available reply is recorded together with what it still costs. The reason is that a reply which succeeds by giving up the position's original advantage has not left the debate where it found it. Two particular inferences are barred by standing rule, because they recur in this literature. Explanatory dissatisfaction is not treated as proof of impossibility. Coherence is not treated as evidence of actuality. Two further boundary rules govern the components most exposed to overreach. Subjective timelessness reported in experience does not by itself establish ontological timelessness. And information preserved in any substrate, a proposed universal mind included, does not by itself establish a continuing personal subject.

Every decisive claim and argument carries a counterevidence entry. The entry is one of three things: an actual opposing observation or argument, an alternative explanation with expectations that differ, or a documented unsuccessful search with a note that coverage remains unresolved. The requirement is for genuine opposition, not for manufactured balance. No quota of objections is invented where the record contains none. A documented failure to find counterevidence is recorded as just that, with its search preserved. It is not recorded as positive support.

Search, inclusion and the frozen literature cutoff

Evidence gathering was staged. The project began with two deliberately narrow pilots: a single named near-death case dossier and a single philosophical argument exchange. Each was independently reviewed, and the stated purpose of both was to test the limits of retrieval, extraction and inference before anything was expanded. Method defects found in the pilots were recorded and repaired at a gate before broader assignments went out. The repairs closed procedural defects. They did not close the pilots' evidence gaps, several of which remain open in the project's register. Later tasks proceeded under bounded contracts covering prospective studies, additional cases, historical and cross-cultural reports, comparative physiological states and the remaining philosophical domains, and a later gate audited the accumulated coverage before any synthesis. The pilots supply no population estimate and no representative sample. The staged structure is a fact about workflow, not a fact about evidence. As of 13 September 2026, forty-one of the forty-six Phase 2 assignments stood accepted. Those are forty-one completed procedures. They are not forty-one independent observations. The remaining five closed on 15 September 2026 at the release adjudication recorded in state/acceptance_P3G2.json: the three synthesis assignments at their rebased scope, the final review and the release gate. The same caution applies to the full forty-six.

Searches followed a grid of query families fixed in advance. The grid covered named cases and their documented challenges, prospective studies and their null results, retrospective and cross-cultural collections, distressing and conflicting reports, and comparative physiological states. It also covered the qualia and emergence literature, neural intervention evidence, existence and fine-tuning arguments, time and identity, meaning and purpose, and moral foundations. Each contract selected its rows and set its allocation of queries before retrieval began. Two balance rules applied throughout. Searches used challenging terms as well as neutral and favorable ones. And named-case tasks reserved queries for documentation, for serious challenge, and for reply or alternative account, so that no case entered the record accompanied only by its friendly retellings. Where no source could be included for a stratum, that stratum is represented by its executed search and an explicit gap. It is never represented by a substitute dressed up as representative.

Screening was bounded and recorded. The default window was the first twenty results an engine actually exposed for each executed query, or all of the exposed results if there were fewer. Favorable and unfavorable queries were screened identically, ranking and visible identifiers were preserved, and risks of truncation were marked. Ranked search results are not a random sample, and no claim in the thesis treats them as one. For each included original source the protocol requires one backward-reference pass and one forward-citation search, where these are accessible within budget, with candidates and reasons for selection logged. That requirement was not met everywhere. The frozen protocol itself records that the passes omitted by the opening comparison task are expressly not counted as executed, and later gate records note further incomplete passes. The recorded synthesis constraints add a standing limit: bounded title queries are discovery, never a complete cited-by index. And discovering a reference is not inspecting it. A source found in another paper's bibliography remains an unverified lead until its relevant text has actually been examined.

The audit trail is meant to run from query to conclusion, and the protocol's third rule says what that requires. Every executed search is to be logged with its date, the destination actually used, the query string as typed, the filters, the candidates, the inclusions, the exclusions and the limits on access. That is the rule. The record does not meet it everywhere, and the register says exactly where it fails. The search work of the earliest comparison task (T03) was lost. The two pilot batches were run mixed, so their per-query ranks cannot be recovered. And one combined forward query (the T05 OR query) was never captured. These are historical facts about the capture, and the register files them as a permanent audit loss: any replacement search carries a new date and repairs nothing about the old record. The rule binds everything else that was logged, and one of its particulars deserves emphasis. The destination logged is the one actually queried; a web index of a database's pages is not recorded as a search of that database. Candidate sources may receive identifiers while they remain unverified leads, and uninspected candidates are never counted as inspected sources. Statements made in conversation and inherited references likewise start as unverified leads, whatever their apparent authority.

What was included? Material that addressed a contracted question and provided one of the following: an original empirical report, study methods and results, a relevant primary historical account, an explicit philosophical argument, or a substantive criticism or reply. Null, unfavorable, distressing and conflicting results were included under identical relevance criteria. That requirement matters in a literature whose popular stratum is dominated by favorable narratives. Firsthand narrative evidence was kept despite gaps in its documentation, and it was labeled by what it can establish. Secondary accounts, interviews and surveys could orient a search, locate originals, or establish that a statement was made. They do not stand in for missing original records.

What was excluded? Material was excluded as independent evidence on only four grounds: it was an exact duplicate, it was a retelling with no new observation, it was irrelevant to the contracted question, or its substantive claims were inaccessible. In the last case the lead and the access gap are preserved, because exclusion for inaccessibility is not a finding that the claim is false. Nothing was excluded for religious affiliation, advocacy, author credentials or commercial motive alone. Such facts were recorded as considerations of provenance that bear on weight. They were not gates on admission.

Standards of inspection were set by what a claim needs. For decisive claims the preference is for original studies and authors' own arguments, inspected at the very passage, with page, section, table or paragraph locators, and together with the methods, results and limitations needed to interpret that passage. An abstract or a snippet is not enough for any claim that rests on denominators or methods no one has seen. Partial access is labeled precisely, and secondhand inspection is never upgraded. When an author quotes a medical chart, the record says that the quotation was inspected, not the chart. Quotation is kept within the applicable limits, and paraphrase with precise locators is preferred.

The literature base is frozen by publication date, not by a stop-clock on searching. The corpus runs from the earliest retrievable publications through 11 September 2026 inclusive, with no lower bound on publication date. For each source, the event date, the report date, the publication date and the retrieval date are recorded separately, and the searches themselves carry their actual execution dates in the logs. Material published after the cutoff is logged as an update lead; it is not quietly mixed into the frozen corpus. Any later extension of the cutoff is a prospective decision of a task contract, logged separately. The language scope is English full texts and accessible English translations, with the original language and the provenance of the translation recorded. Where access to a translation remains unresolved, that is a coverage gap, not evidence that no relevant literature exists, and the English constraint is itself a recorded bias on any cultural generalization. Under these constraints the project is a bounded review with systematic procedures. It is not an exhaustive or completed systematic review, and it does not describe itself as one.

The method also fixes what was never authorized. There were no payments for access, no contact with witnesses or authors, no requests for private records, and no external messages in the course of evidence work. Where a primary text could not be reached by public routes, the routes attempted and the need left unmet were recorded, and the work continued elsewhere. Instructions embedded in source documents were treated as untrusted data throughout.

Independent review and what acceptance means

The unit of work is a contracted task, and each task passes through four hands. An overseer issues a filled contract that specifies the question, the exclusions, the inputs by exact hash, the outputs, the identifiers, the budget and the acceptance criteria. A researcher submits a packet containing the report, the proposed ledger records and a result envelope. A reviewer who did not write the packet examines it. And the overseer adjudicates. Only the overseer writes the canonical ledgers, and proposed records enter them only after review. Every contract, packet and review identifies the protocol version and the exact hashes of its inputs. A later reader can therefore determine precisely which version of which document a finding rests on.

Review is inspection, not endorsement. The reviewer reopens every source that supports a decisive claim, checks the cited passages together with the surrounding methods and context needed to interpret them, and samples the supporting material. Review also verifies query coverage against the contract, the reasons for selection and exclusion, accessibility labels, denominator units, the handling of duplicates and overlap, and chronology. It verifies the strongest alternatives, the counterevidence entries, the bridge premises, and the separation of the three confidence judgments described below. Reviews run under their own budgets, with a default cap and scoped extensions when decisive sources exceed it. A decisive source is never quietly skipped to save budget.

The verdicts have fixed meanings. Accept means that the contracted method was met, and this explicitly includes the case where the substantive result is an honestly unresolved question. Acceptance is a statement about procedure. It is never a statement about metaphysical truth. Revise means a material defect that can be repaired with bounded corrections. Unresolved means that decisive evidence is inaccessible or that a substantive disagreement persists, and the review says which conclusion is limited as a result. Four defects block unconditional acceptance outright: a decisive citation error, a concealed missing denominator, an overcount of independence, and an unmarked inference from report to event. The chapters of this thesis take these adjudicated statuses as they stand. They do not improve on them.

At most two substantive revision cycles are permitted per task. After that, the disagreement is recorded or the task is split, so that iteration cannot substitute for resolution. No author approves their own output. Reviewers report directly to the overseer, not to authors. Disagreements are preserved in the record rather than reconciled by fiat. And agreement among the models used to execute tasks supplies no additional empirical observation. Two systems endorsing the same claim is one claim, and the project draws no support from unanimity as such.

Findings are versioned. A change of method creates a numbered protocol version with a recorded decision that says what changed, why it changed, and which earlier tasks need reassessment. When an accepted upstream finding changes, the downstream tasks it affects are marked stale or blocked pending review. They do not quietly keep conclusions built on the earlier version. The submitted and the reviewed versions of every packet are preserved. New original data and repaired claims require fresh review by a distinct author; they do not take over the acceptance of what they replace.

These meanings of acceptance bound what the thesis can claim for itself. Its findings are accepted within a recorded project procedure, by AI-assisted authors and reviewers under an overseer. They have not undergone journal peer review, and nothing in this document turns procedural acceptance into external validation. When a chapter reports that a claim is supported, the term names the project's recorded judgment with its recorded scope. It does not name a fact about the consensus of any research community.

Tracking dependence and preventing double counting

How is double counting prevented? It begins with sources. Sources are deduplicated by stable identifier first, and then by normalized title, authors, year and content. Preprints, published versions, corrections and supplements are linked to one another, and versions are distinguished wherever a relevant assertion changes between them. The reason is plain: an argument quietly strengthened between editions is a different argument. The same article hosted in different places is one source with several routes of access. It is not several sources, any more than ten copies of one photograph are ten photographs.

Cases are deduplicated by the underlying person, event, date, site and chronology. Books, interviews, sermons and copied stories about one episode share a duplicate group and count once. A widely retold case is one case, however large its literature. Distinct episodes involving one person are recorded as related; they are not assumed to be independent. Apparent corroboration gets the same scrutiny. A new witness is tagged with relationship, exposure to information and the timing of the statement. Several witnesses repeating one briefing are not independent corroboration. They are one account with several voices.

Studies and samples are checked for overlap by recruitment sites, enrollment dates, registry identifiers, participant counts and the authors' own statements about overlap. Secondary analyses, pooled studies and follow-ups are linked to the cohorts beneath them. Sample sizes are never added together where it has not been established that the samples are disjoint. Risks of dependence are tracked even where the participants differ, because shared recruitment channels and the cultural transmission of narrative motifs can entangle collections that are formally distinct. The same discipline extends to philosophy. Arguments that share a premise carrying real weight are assigned to a dependency group. The cumulative chapter therefore cannot count one premise's plausibility several times under several names.

Rates and counts keep their anatomy. Every empirical rate keeps its numerator, its denominator, its unit of observation, its definition of eligibility, its timeframe, its exclusions and its missingness, with an exact locator. An unknown is never replaced with zero. A missing target opportunity is never scored as a failed test. Prospective studies are extracted along their full flow: from eligible events through enrollment, survival, eligibility for interview, consent, completed interviews, classified experiences, monitored cases and exposure to a target, to attempts at identification. Categories that a study does not report as nested are not forced into a single chain of attrition. Proportions are computed only from compatible units over justified denominators. Study-specific rates are kept before any aggregation, and pooling requires a separately justified contract.

Double counting is controlled, finally, at the level of use. A single claim may bear on several hypothesis components, but it remains one observation however many components it touches. Within this thesis the control is built into the structure. Every substantive paragraph is mapped in a crosswalk to the specific claim, argument, case and source records, or the labeled premises, on which it rests. Reuse of one record across chapters is therefore visible and checkable instead of being diffused through the prose. The dependence map of the cumulative chapter draws on these crosswalks, and the whole-thesis review checks them for this very failure.

Sensitivity testing and restraint about numbers

Three assessments are kept separate for every claim, and they are never averaged. Record reliability asks whether this particular observation, report, quotation or attribution is documented with usable provenance, and it says whether the judgment applies to the existence of a report or to the event the report describes. Inference strength asks how well a particular conclusion follows from the record when serious alternatives are considered, with the bridge premises, the dependence and any decisive missing information stated. Component confidence asks what provisional assessment each of the nine hypothesis components warrants, given the reviewed evidence and objections. Each assessment uses the qualitative labels supported, contested, unresolved or challenged, with reasons and scope. The labels are not numerical bins. No arithmetic over them is defined, and none is used.

These separations do real work. A reliable narrative may support only a weak metaphysical inference. That an account is well documented, early, stable and sincere settles nothing about whether its best explanation is exotic. An accurate attribution of a philosophical premise to its author is not evidence that the premise is true. And a claim's reliability label is never passed on as confidence in a hypothesis component. A record only supports a component through bridge premises that have to be stated, and those premises can themselves be contested. Nor is a task-level unresolved label converted into a cumulative prior, or into equal support for competing families.

Numerical probabilities are permitted, but they are disciplined. They require explicitly specified hypotheses, defensible ranges for priors and likelihoods, stated assumptions about dependence, and sensitivity analysis. Overlapping metaphysical families are not forced to add up to one hundred percent, because the families are neither exclusive nor exhaustive in the way that would require. Where defensible likelihoods are unavailable, and in this evidence base they generally are unavailable, the thesis offers conditional qualitative comparisons and says plainly that the evidence does not identify a numerical posterior. No pooled posterior over cases or samples appears anywhere in this document. The denominators, the disjointness and the likelihoods that such a number would require were not established.

Robustness is tested by withdrawal, not by weighting. The article's qualitative rule is simple: remove particular cases or premises and ask which reasons remain. The cumulative chapter carries that rule as eight prespecified sensitivity tests, executed at the accepted synthesis stage and re-read in the cumulative chapter against the fuller chapters. The first removes the strongest disputed named near-death case, selected by an explicit criterion rather than by convenience, with a second candidate shown wherever the ordering of strength is ambiguous. The second omits all retrospective cases and narratives, the retrospective community arm included, while keeping the truly prospective observations at their real denominators. The third removes contributions whose sample or event dependence is uncertain. The fourth varies the criteria of personal identity across psychological, organismic and simple-subject views, sets strict identity against prudential continuity, and varies four of the recorded time-and-identity models. The fifth removes the central bridge premises: endurance required for value, unity to obligation, and consciousness to authority. The sixth removes the global sufficient-reason premise, the epistemic-to-metaphysical premise and the life-valuing designer-preference premise, one at a time. The seventh removes the strongest selected neural-intervention constraint or report-to-experience proxy while keeping the independence model's actual predictive constraints, so that the countercase is stress-tested with the same severity as the case. The eighth restricts the comparison to directly inspected positive original defenders and their direct replies, and it excludes project reconstructions from any claim about authors. Each test reports its effect on each of the nine components separately.

Stronger conclusions are gated, not hedged. The recorded synthesis constraints require every stronger intended conclusion to be mapped to the specific gap that blocks it. The project's register records thirty-five gap groups, each with an explicit condition for closing it, together with twelve withheld stronger-conclusion gates. All twelve are currently withheld. Before any chapter of this thesis asserts one of those conclusions, the blocking gap must have been repaired under review; otherwise the conclusion is explicitly withheld and its gate is cited. A generic caveat does not license a gated verdict. Appendix C carries the full register of dispositions, and the concluding chapters say which gates remain closed and what that costs the argument.

What this method cannot deliver

What can this method not deliver? Begin with coverage, which is bounded and asymmetric. Searches were budgeted, the screening windows were finite, the corpus is frozen at the publication cutoff, and the language scope skews cultural coverage toward material accessible in English. Several of these bounds are sharper than a reader of the protocol's requirements alone would infer. The destinations named in the search grid were planned venues, not claims of access to a database. Where a database was unavailable, the logs record a public web index queried in its place, and the two are not treated as the same thing. Engine rankings change over time and cannot be reproduced exactly; the logs preserve what was visible, not an experiment that can be rerun. And the obligation to run backward- and forward-citation passes was not met everywhere, beginning with the recorded opening exception. Nor can the historic searches be reconstructed. The lost early search work, the mixed pilot ranks and the uncaptured combined query described earlier are permanent losses, and no later search, however carefully logged, restores them. The citation network around the included sources is therefore bounded discovery, not a completed map. Nor is every position represented by an inspected original defender. The record distinguishes inspected originals, secondary attributions, project reconstructions and uninspected variants, and the comparative verdicts are restricted to match. In particular, no broad ranking of worldview families is asserted where a rival's strongest formulation was never directly inspected. Conclusions of that shape are either narrowed to the formulations actually examined or withheld under the register described above.

The layers of enforcement and review have honest limits of their own. The closed taxonomies this chapter describes, six epistemic types and four assessment labels, bind as rules and as review criteria. But the ledger fields that carry them are free text, and they have drifted into a much larger vocabulary. Discipline lives in the judgment of reviewers, not in a schema that rejects a mislabeled record. The independent review this chapter describes is review by a distinct AI model context, under the same overseer, protocol and toolchain as the author. It is not re-inspection by human specialists. It includes no access to medical charts or private records, which no route in this project could authorize. The contracted independent final review of the article that this thesis expands was never performed as a single review. Its obligations passed, without weakening, into this thesis's chapter reviews and whole-thesis review, and the release adjudication of 15 September 2026 found the two layers jointly sufficient (state/acceptance_P3G2.json). That closure is methodological, like every acceptance in this project, and the article's own conclusions keep their working-draft status; promoting the article is a separate owner decision that has not been taken. The eight sensitivity tests were specified in full at the synthesis audit, which came after the first phase of the evidence gathering and before the second. They are prespecified relative to the cumulative assessment they constrain, not relative to the collection of evidence. Finally, the public snapshot of this project excludes downloaded source files and raw audit materials. A public reader can verify the structure of the record and the reasoning. That reader cannot replay the inspections themselves.

Let me be clear, finally, about what this method has not shown and cannot show. It cannot turn its own rigor into truth. Procedural acceptance, however disciplined, is a fact about this project's workflow. The requirements that a distinct reviewer reopen decisive sources and that disagreements be recorded raise the cost of error; they do not abolish it. The assessments that follow are qualitative, comparative and conditional. They identify no numerical posterior, certify no completed systematic review, and claim no external peer validation. What the discipline of this chapter establishes is a requirement, not an achievement. Every substantive conclusion in the following chapters must be traceable through the crosswalk to identified records and labeled premises, testable by the stated withdrawals, and checkable against the stated gates. A reader who rejects a conclusion can then locate which record, premise or bridge they are rejecting. Whether each chapter meets that requirement is decided by its own independent review, not by this chapter's description of the rule. And all twelve withheld stronger-conclusion gates remain withheld through this chapter, which opens none of them and asserts no conclusion they reserve.

3The Space of Alternatives

This chapter is the thesis's literature review. Its job is to set out the six families of positions against which every later assessment is made. They are physicalism with its illusionist edge, psychophysical-law and strong-emergence views, panpsychism, substance dualism, idealism in its universal and plural forms, and theism. Each family is presented through the strongest formulation available in the project record, together with its strongest recorded challenge and, where one was inspected, the actual reply. Let me be careful about that word "strongest." "Strongest available" is a bounded claim about this record, not about the literature as a whole. For that reason every position carries a coverage mark. It entered the record in one of four ways: as an inspected original text, as a secondary attribution reported through another inspected text, as a project reconstruction built for comparison, or as an uninspected variant known only as a lead. These marks do real work. The recorded synthesis constraints bar any broad ranking of families wherever a rival's strongest formulation was never directly inspected. They permit at most an explicitly narrower comparison of the formulations actually examined. And the frozen protocol's own header records a standing limit on the whole space, which I take up at the close of this chapter.

The material comes in two layers, and the reader should keep them apart. At the project's opening, each family received what the project calls an operational comparator. This is a deliberately schematic model that stipulates four things: the family's required commitments, its principal explanatory bet, its strongest generic objection and its strongest generic reply. These comparators are project reconstructions. They were reviewed as method rather than as scholarship, and no philosopher is responsible for them. On top of them, the evidence tasks assembled developed exchanges: original defenses, direct objections and actual replies, each inspected at an identified version with locators. This chapter presents both layers and keeps them distinct. Why? Because a schematic model can be tidier than any position a philosopher actually holds, and that tidiness is itself a risk of distortion. Nothing in this chapter ranks the families. The comparison's verdict-bearing work happens downstream, under gates that remain withheld.

Physicalist identity and the illusionist program

Begin with the operational physicalist comparator. Its bet is that a complete physical realization account of experience is possible. The bridge that carries the bet is the principle that conceptual differences need not establish ontological differences. Its stipulated strongest objection is that phenomenal character may remain unexplained when every functional fact is in. Its stipulated reply is that identity or grounding need not be conceptually transparent. That is all a reconstruction can do: it sets the family's shape through a stipulated bet, objection and reply. The motivation recorded with the comparator is economy, in the sense that no extra ontological laws are required.

The developed identity defense in the record is Papineau's. On the inspected text, he argues that a mind–brain identity can be warranted a posteriori, without the mental being derivable a priori from the physical. He argues further that an identity, once warranted, needs no further explanation. There is no residual question of why a thing is itself [S-T05-005]. What becomes of the famous felt gap? Papineau relocates it. On his account it is not evidence of two properties but a psychological resistance to identification, and the causes of that resistance are open to empirical investigation. The project's conceptual analysis adds the discipline this exchange forces, and the discipline cuts both ways. A justified identity differs from an assumed one. And a conceptual gap alone neither refutes nor establishes ontological identity.

Two working definitions from the record frame everything that follows. Weak emergence means that experience is completely grounded in the physical, with no new basic experiential law. Strong emergence adds irreducible properties or laws, and in that form it can overlap with property dualism. That line is the real divide. The families in this chapter differ, at bottom, on where they place experience relative to it, and on what they are prepared to pay for their placement.

Illusionism is the record's developed program for answering the gap by denying its target. It does not try to close the gap. It denies the reality of the thing the gap seems to separate us from. Frankish's strong illusionism, on the inspected author version, denies genuinely qualitative phenomenal properties altogether, whether physical or nonphysical. It keeps experience in an inclusive sense: perceptual states, access, reports and introspective representations are all real, and none of them is phenomenally qualitative [S-P2T05-001]. The central task then becomes explaining why systems like us represent ourselves as having qualitative states. Three motivations are recorded: conservative explanation, introspective fallibility and an abduction from the anomalousness of phenomenal properties. It should be said plainly that the account is a program with proposed content mechanisms, not a completed theory. The inspected text itself says so.

The direct challenge in the record is Nida-Rümelin's. Her text was inspected as an author manuscript; the final print was not available for byte-level or visual verification. On that manuscript, the illusionist has misdrawn the target from the start. Phenomenal consciousness concerns subjects instantiating experiential properties, not inner events bearing theoretical "phenomenal properties." The relevant properties are introduced by examples, so their reference does not depend on any philosophical description an illusionist could reject [S-P2T05-002]. Her positive premise is simple to state. To undergo an experiential property is already to be aware of it. Awareness is the instantiation itself, not a second, error-prone introspective act aimed at it. Two qualifications belong here. Her anti-illusionist conclusion is expressly conditional on at least one experiential example actually being instantiated. And she notes, but sets aside, the popular quick objection that an illusion of experience would itself have to be experienced.

Frankish's actual reply was inspected in the same exchange. He grants the recasting in terms of subjects and properties for the sake of argument, and he accepts the ordinary examples. What he denies is that reference by example fixes the realist nature of what is referred to. The shared feature the examples pick out may admit a representational, nonphenomenal interpretation. Cognitive access to structural physical properties requires mediation, so direct acquaintance cannot, by his lights, settle the question [S-P2T05-003]. He is candid about where this move comes from. His rejection of robust acquaintance flows from his physicalist commitments, and he does not expect it to persuade this critic. The reply also corrects an earlier causal shorthand: it is representations, not their nonexistent intentional objects, that produce effects. It distinguishes illusionism from physicalism itself, and from any blanket claim that experience judgments are unreliable. He even concedes that Russellian realism need not contradict science; he prefers illusionism on explanatory grounds alone.

Where does the exchange rest? It is recorded as unresolved, and the open question is this: would an adequate functional account preserve the intended target, or would it quietly change the subject? There is also the regress worry. Each apparent feel seems to need a higher representation, which threatens either a circle or a concealed phenomenal starting point. The record treats this as a real explanatory constraint rather than an automatic contradiction, because the illusionist may allow fine-grained nonphenomenal seemings and a limited higher-order stop. Each side rests on a premise that carries the weight of its case: the adequacy of the functional account on one side, the identification of awareness with instantiation on the other. Both remain what they are. They are premises.

Strong emergence and psychophysical laws

The operational psychophysical-law comparator begins with a concession: additional experiential resources may be required beyond the physical inventory. Its bet is a constrained mapping. Fundamental laws link physical organization to experience, and they explain instances of experience without explaining their own existence. Its stipulated objection is that the central dependence stays brute. Its stipulated reply is that every model needs stopping points, and that the same explanatory stopping rules should apply across models. The anchoring attribution in the record is Chalmers's proposal of fundamental experience with supplementary psychophysical principles, which its author describes as naturalistic dualism [S-T03-001]. The inspected text concedes that this does not explain the existence of basic experience.

The recorded costs of this family are two, one causal and one epistemic. Under physical causal closure and a ban on systematic overdetermination, a distinct experiential influence threatens to be excluded. And if experience does not cause our reports, epiphenomenalism threatens any knowledge based on experience. The mapped replies split the family. One variant appeals to lawful tracking and direct acquaintance, with no extra physical effects. The other questions closure and proposes interaction laws. The comparison packet that mapped this split recorded, within its own scope, that the interaction and substance variants then lacked inspected direct chains of defender and reply. A later task repaired part of that remainder. The substance-dualism section below therefore rests on directly inspected Lowe, Engelhardt and Bennett texts, including Engelhardt's interactive inclusive variant. What still has no inspected defense of its own, within this chapter's inputs, is a Cartesian-style two-way interactionism. It appears only by contrast, in the non-Cartesian label of the inspected position. Two project analyses set the outer limits of the family's reach. First, property dualism alone supplies no detachable subject and no survival condition, since instantiation can remain tied to an organized substrate. Second, a closure-based exclusion argument needs three premises: sufficient physical causation, mental causal relevance and no overdetermination. Energy conservation alone is not that argument.

Causal emergence is the strong-emergence wing of the family, and the record develops it through an inspected exchange. O'Connor's 1994 formulation required supervenience, nonstructurality and downward causal influence [S-P2T01-003]. The 2005 account with Wong replaces it. That account posits basic nonstructural properties that organized physical systems causally generate and sustain over time; it rejects general synchronic supervenience, and it explicitly repudiates the earlier formulation [S-P2T01-001]. The revision matters for everything downstream. On this model, experience is a caused, maintained and genuinely novel feature of certain systems. It consists of basic nonstructural properties, every instance of which depends on organized matter. What this means for fundamentality is deferred to its recorded conditional form below.

The selected challenge is Kim's preemption argument. It is worth noticing what Kim allows. On the inspected reprint, he allows ordinary downward causation, and he treats phenomenal qualia as promising candidates for irreducibility. His challenge is narrower and sharper than a blanket denial. Where an emergent has a sufficient noncausal basal condition, the base threatens to preempt the emergent as a cause, and merely relocating the emergent's effects later in time does not remove the problem [S-P2T01-002]. The argument has premises of its own: noncausal determination, basal sufficiency and exclusion. They are substantive, and the record treats them as such.

The 2005 reply meets the argument at its noncausal premise. If the base causally generates the emergent, then the emergent is an intermediate cause in a temporal process, not a redundant competitor. Take away that disputed premise and the preemption inference fails against this model. The inspected text is honest about what the reply costs. It costs a disputed simplicity comparison against latent-power alternatives. It costs a history-dependent thought experiment that stipulates its own discriminating conditions. And it costs a contested first-person motivation. No independently confirmed emergence law is supplied, and the record notes the omission.

Shoemaker's countercase shows that one can resist Kim without the nonstructural ontology. On the inspected article, he appeals to micro-latent powers: powers of ordinary microconstituents that are manifested only in suitable, emergence-engendering combinations. These powers let the complete microfacts fix the macrofacts, while keeping causal power distinct from mere nomic necessitation. Shoemaker considers a distinct-property option and rejects it, because he is committed to essential causal individuation [S-P2T01-004]. The project's comparison point follows from this. To decide between the temporal-emergence reply and the latent-powers reply, one must adjudicate property individuation, dependence relations and specified empirical discriminators. Counting successful replies to exclusion will not do it.

What does this family buy? On the record, something definite and strictly bounded.
